--skip-git-repo-check --model gpt-6.1-sol --json --enable unified_exec -- '# Minified code does not behave like the source  The repository at `/app` is SWC on its main branch. Users of SWC'\"'\"'s JavaScript minifier (`swc_ecma_minifier`, the minifier behind `jsc.minify` and `@swc/core`'\"'\"'s `minify()`) have reported several programs that do something different once minified. Each report below reproduces on this commit with the compressor'\"'\"'s default optimizations (`defaults: true`) and the options it names.  1. **Imported bindings.** An ES module import is a live binding: when the exporting module changes the exported variable, the importer sees the new value. The minifier treats imported bindings as if they never change. With `counter.js` being `export let count = 0; export function bump() { count++; }`:     ```js    import { count, bump } from \"./counter.js\";     export function changed(f) {        const before = count;        f();        return before !== count;    }     console.log(changed(bump), changed(() => {}));    ```     prints `true false`; the minified module prints `false false`.  2. **Default parameter values.** A parameter default that names a function or object defined outside the function must evaluate to that same function or object on every call:     ```js    (function () {        function load(value) { return value + 1; }        function hook([callback = load] = []) { return callback; }        globalThis.render = function (callbacks) { return hook(callbacks); };    })();    console.log(render() === render([]));    ```     prints `true`; the minified script prints `false`.  3. **`super` method calls.** Inside a class or object-literal method, `(0, super.method)()` calls the method with `this` undefined, unlike `super.method()`. The minifier rewrites the first form into the second, so the method receives the current object.  4. **Inferred names with `keep_fnames` / `keep_classnames`.** With the compress option `keep_fnames: true`, `var f = (0, function () {});` gives `f.name === \"\"`, but the minified code gives the function the name `\"f\"`. The same happens wherever JavaScript would infer a name for an anonymous function, arrow function or class (with `keep_classnames: true` for classes) when the source writes it as `(0, ...)`.  5. **Classes that refer to themselves, with `mangle: { keep_classnames: true }`:**     ```js    (() => {        class Foo {            static self = Foo;        }        console.log(Foo.self === Foo);    })();    ```     prints `true`; the minified script throws a `ReferenceError`.  6. **Constant factors.** `x * 24 * 60 * 60` is minified to `24 * x * 3600`. Floating-point multiplication is not associative: for `x = 365.2425` the source computes `31556951.999999996` and the minified code `31556952`.  Fix the minifier so that none of these reports reproduces and, more generally, so that for every program of these six kinds the minified program behaves exactly as the source program does when run: the same output, the same values and the same exceptions. This holds for any compress and mangle options apart from those that let the minifier assume something the program does not guarantee (such as the `unsafe*` options), including when the minifier'\"'\"'s own output is minified again. As today, the minifier may change the `name` of a function or class unless `keep_fnames` or `keep_classnames` asks to keep it. The shape and size of the minified code are free. The change must hold for every program of these six kinds, not for particular names or inputs. Everything else that works on this commit today must keep working.  Make your change only under `crates/swc_ecma_minifier/src`, `crates/swc_ecma_ast/src`, `crates/swc_ecma_transforms_base/src`, `crates/swc_ecma_transforms_optimization/src` and `crates/swc_ecma_utils/src`; leave every `tests/` directory, `Cargo.toml` files, `Cargo.lock` and build files unchanged. The environment has no network access. Every crate `Cargo.lock` names is already in `CARGO_HOME`, so `cargo build` and `cargo test` run offline; `cargo test -p swc_ecma_minifier --no-run` takes about two minutes at 8 CPUs from a cold start, and `node` is installed for the minifier'\"'\"'s tests that run the minified code. Place these tests in the parser or decorator transform suites instead.\n\n### Minifier Semantic Assumptions\n\nThe ECMAScript minifier makes the following assumptions to make minification\nefficient, as documented in the [SWC minification guide](https://swc.rs/docs/configuration/minification#assumptions).\n\n- `.toString()` and `.valueOf()` have no side effects. For built-in objects,\n  these methods have not been overridden.\n- `undefined`, `NaN`, and `Infinity` have not been externally redefined.\n- `arguments.callee`, `arguments.caller`, and `Function.prototype.caller` are\n  not used. These are not valid in strict mode.\n- Code does not depend on the exact contents of `Function.prototype.toString()`\n  or `Error.prototype.stack`.\n- Getting and setting properties on a plain object does not cause other side\n  effects through `.watch()` or `Proxy`.\n- Object properties can be added, removed, and modified. These operations are\n  not prevented by `Object.defineProperty()`, `Object.defineProperties()`,\n  `Object.freeze()`, `Object.preventExtensions()`, or `Object.seal()`.\n- `document.all` is not `null`.\n- Assigning properties to a class has no side effects and does not throw.\n- Accessing declared top-level identifiers has no side effects. If a variable\n  is declared on `globalThis` using a getter with side effects, the minifier\n  will likely break it.\n- Temporal dead zone (TDZ) violations do not exist. The minifier ignores TDZ\n  violations and does not preserve them.\n- Arithmetic expressions may be treated as having no side effects. Code must\n  not rely on exceptions from arithmetic operations, such as adding a `bigint`\n  and a `number`:\n\n  ```js\n  const a = 1n;\n  const b = 1;\n  const c = a + b;\n  ```\n\n  The JavaScript engine throws an exception when it executes this code, but the\n  minifier ignores that exception.\n\nRelated: [Terser compiler assumptions](https://github.com/terser/terser#compiler-assumptions).\n\n#### Implementation and Review Policy\n\nThe following is a project policy for minifier implementation and review,\nseparate from the public documentation assumptions listed above:\n\n- Prioritize correctness for common usage, performance, and simple, maintainable\n  minifier code.\n- Do not require additional defensive logic or analysis solely to preserve\n  extreme edge cases involving monkey-patched globals or built-in objects,\n  unusual reassignment of `arguments.length`, bindings introduced by direct\n  `eval`, or dynamic name resolution inside `with`.\n- Semantic differences that depend only on these cases are not mandatory review\n  fixes. A required fix must demonstrate an impact on common usage or identify an\n  explicit support contract that the change violates.\n- Continue to account for ordinary lexical shadowing, side effects, and\n  exceptions within the supported assumptions. This policy does not authorize\n  blanket removal of existing `eval` or `with` handling or tests.\n\nIf a minifier change relies on an assumption beyond this list and policy, update the\npublic minification documentation or leave an explicit code comment describing\nthe narrower local invariant. extract_class_side_effect<'a>(\n    expr_ctx: ExprCtx,\n    ident: Option<&'a Ident>,\n    c: &'a mut Class,\n) -> Option<Vec<&'a mut Box<Expr>>> {\n    let mut res = Vec::new();\n    let mut value = Vec::new();\n    if let Some(e) = &mut c.super_class {\n        if e.may_have_side_effects(expr_ctx) {\n            res.push(e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    let mut visitor = ClassEffectVisitor {\n        found: false,\n        private_ident: FxHashSet::default(),\n        nested_class_depth: 0,\n    };\n\n    for m in &mut c.body {\n        if let ClassMember::PrivateProp(PrivateProp { key, .. })\n        | ClassMember::PrivateMethod(PrivateMethod { key, .. })\n        | ClassMember::AutoAccessor(AutoAccessor {\n            key: Key::Private(key),\n            ..\n        }) = m\n        {\n            visitor.private_ident.insert(key.name.clone());\n        }\n    }\n\n    for m in &mut c.body {\n        match m {\n            ClassMember::Method(ClassMethod {\n                key: PropName::Computed(key),\n                ..\n            }) => {\n                if key.expr.may_have_side_effects(expr_ctx) {\n                    res.push(&mut key.expr);\n                }\n            }\n\n            ClassMember::ClassProp(p) => {\n                if let PropName::Computed(key) = &mut p.key {\n                    if key.expr.may_have_side_effects(expr_ctx) {\n {\n                        return None;\n                    }\n\n                    if let Some(id) = ident {\n                        if IdentUsageFinder::find(id, v) {\n                            return None;\n                        }\n                    }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Returns true when a class member is safely discardable by\n/// `extract_class_side_effect`.\nfn is_discardable_class_default_member(member: &ClassMember) -> bool {\n    match member {\n        ClassMember::Method(method) => !method.key.is_computed(),\n        ClassMember::ClassProp(prop) => !prop.key.is_computed(),\n        ClassMember::AutoAccessor(accessor) => {\n            // Static auto-accessor initializers run while evaluating the class,\n            // but the class-side-effect extractor cannot reconstruct them.\n            (!accessor.is_static || accessor.value.is_none())\n                && !matches!(&accessor.key, Key::Public(key) if key.is_computed())\n        }\n in_class_static_initializer: bool,\n}\n\nfn has_observable_param_initialization(pat: &Pat) -> bool {\n    match pat {\n        Pat::Ident(..) => false,\n        Pat::Rest(rest) => has_observable_param_initialization(&rest.arg),\n        _ => true,\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Visit for NonDiscardableDefaultVisitor {\n    noop_visit_type!();\n\n    fn visit_assign_expr(&mut self, e: &AssignExpr) {\n        if self.in_class_static_initializer {\n            // Class static initializers run in strict mode. Extracting an assignment\n            // into the surrounding script can turn a ReferenceError into a global\n            // property creation, so retain the class instead.\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        e.visit_children_with(self);\n    }\n\n    fn visit_update_expr(&mut self, e: &UpdateExpr) {\n        if self.in_class_static_initializer {\n            // Like assignments, updates of unresolved names throw in a class's\n            // strict-mode static initializer but can create globals when moved.\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        e.visit_children_with(self);\n    }\n\n    fn visit_unary_expr(&mut self, e: &UnaryExpr) {\n        if self.in_class_static_initializer && e.op == op!(\"delete\") {\n            // Class static initializers run in strict mode. Moving a deletion into\n            // a surrounding sloppy script can turn its TypeError into `false`.\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        e.visit_children_with(self);\n    }\n\n    fn visit_arrow_expr(&mut self, e: &ArrowExpr) {\n        // Arrows capture `new.target` lexically. A class side-effect extraction\n        // can relocate an arrow without invoking it, which still makes a\n        // captured `new.target` invalid at the relocation site.\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        e.visit_children_with(self);\n    }\n\n    fn visit_class(&mut self, e: &Class) {\n        // Evaluating `class extends value {}` throws unless `value` is a\n        // constructor or `null`. The side-effect extractor intentionally only\n        // keeps expressions with direct effects, so retain the class default\n        // when its heritage cannot be proven to be `null`.\n        if e.super_class\n            .as_deref()\n            .is_some_and(|super_class| !matches!(super_class, Expr::Lit(Lit::Null(..))))\n        {\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        // Computed class keys perform ToPropertyKey coercion. The side-effect\n        // extractor can omit that coercion when the key expression itself is\n        // pure, so retain the whole default instead.\n        if e.body\n            .iter()\n            .any(|member| !is_discardable_class_default_member(member))\n        {\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        for member in &e.body {\n            let initializer = match member {\n                ClassMember::ClassProp(prop) if prop.is_static => prop.value.as_deref(),\n                ClassMember::PrivateProp(prop) if prop.is_static => prop.value.as_deref(),\n                ClassMember::StaticBlock(block) if block.body.stmts.len() == 1 => block\n                    .body\n                    .stmts\n                    .first()\n                    .and_then(|stmt| stmt.as_expr())\n                    .map(|stmt| &*stmt.expr),\n                _ => None,\n            };\n\n            if let Some(initializer) = initializer {\n                let was_in_class_static_initializer = self.in_class_static_initializer;\n                self.in_class_static_initializer = true;\n                initializer.visit_with(self);\n                self.in_class_static_initializer = was_in_class_static_initializer;\n\n                if self.found {\n                    return;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        e.visit_children_with(self);\n    }\n\n    fn visit_member_expr(&mut self, e: &MemberExpr) {\n        if matches!(e.prop, MemberProp::Computed(..)) {\n            // A computed member access performs ToPropertyKey coercion. The class\n            // side-effect extractor can discard that coercion when the key itself\n            // is pure, so retain the default instead.\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        if is_restricted_function_property_access(e) {\n            // Function `arguments` is a restricted property. Reading it can throw\n            // while a function is evaluated in a class initializer, but class-side\n            // effect extraction treats ordinary member reads as discardable.\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        if matches!(e.prop, MemberProp::PrivateName(..)) {\n            // Private member access performs a brand check that can throw even when\n            // evaluating the object expression itself has no observable effects.\n            // `extract_class_side_effect` cannot reproduce that check outside the\n            // class, so keep the class default intact.\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        e.visit_children_with(self);\n    }\n\n    fn visit_call_expr(&mut self, e: &CallExpr) {\n        if e.args.iter().any(|arg| arg.spread.is_some()) {\n            // Spread call arguments invoke the iterator protocol, which cannot be\n            // preserved by class-side-effect extraction.\n            self.found = true;\n            return;\n        }\n\n        if let Callee::Expr(callee) = &e.callee {\n            if is_potentially_throwing_pure_member_call(callee, &e.args) {\n                // `may_have_side_effects` recognizes known string and Math methods as pure,\n                // but their arguments may throw while being coerced. Class-side effect\n                // extraction cannot preserve that exception, so retain the default.\n                self.found = true;\n                return;\n            }\n        }\n\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

This keeps TDZ-observable\n        // cycles such as `class A extends B {} class B extends A {}` (which\n        // throws at runtime) from being eliminated.\n        // The backward-reference optimization (attributing the `extends` edge to\n        // the subclass so an unreachable cycle can collapse) is only applied when\n        // it is provably safe.
